The reports shall be printed in 1264 and 1285 respectively. Therefore, pursuant to Article. 95e of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, I have decided to supplement the agenda with points covering the examination of these reports. The Council of Ministers has submitted a draft law amending the Law on National Tax Administration and the Act on National Tax Administration. The project is print number 1297. Therefore, after obtaining the uniform opinion of the Seniors Convention, I decided to supplement the agenda by a point covering the first reading of this draft law. The Public Finance Commission submitted a report on the government draft law amending the Individual Income Tax Act. The report shall be published in the Official Journal of the European Union. Therefore, after obtaining the uniform opinion of the Seniors Convention, I decided to supplement the agenda by a point covering the examination of this report. In this case, I propose that the Sejm consent to the application of the article. 51 (3) of the Sejm Regulation. If I do not hear opposition, I will think that the Sejm has accepted the offer. I can't hear you objecting. The Sejm Bureau also proposes that the Sejm listen to three minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles in discussions on the draft resolution on the commemoration of the 30th anniversary of the death of Fr. Franciszek Blaclinicki. The Sejm Bureau also proposes that the Sejm should listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circles in discussions on the other items on the agenda. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u70	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a report by the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications about the draft law on amending the Penal Code Act and the Act on Juvenile Procedure, print No 846 presented by the President of the Republic of Poland. Marshal of the Sejm at the 28th meeting of the Sejm on 21 October 2016 pursuant to Article. 87. 2 in conjunction with Article. 90. 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Extraordinary Commission for amendments to the codifications for consideration. The Extraordinary Commission appointed a subcommittee and after considering this draft law at the meetings of the Extraordinary Commission on 3 November 2016 and 26 January 2017 and at the subcommittee meeting on 14 December asks the High Sejm to approve the attached draft law. This law introduces changes concerning the judicial dimension of the penalty, i.e. Article. 53 § 2nd Penal Code. In the original text in print No 846 this article. 53 § 2 was supplemented by another directive on damage to the minor in the course of a criminal offence to be taken into account by the court of its own motion. Following the discussions at these committee meetings, which I have already mentioned, an amendment of this kind was introduced that the provision concerning the offence to the detriment of the minor was amended in such a way that it was extended, using such a wording that another condition would be introduced to this judge's dimension of the penalty for committing the offence to the detriment of the person incompetent due to age or health status. Of course, this already existing condition has been retained, which has an impact on the level of the penalty if the offence has been committed together with the minor. The President's submission also concerns changes in the rules on crime against life and health, against freedom, against family. The sanction of the Article has been tightened. 156 § 1 points 1 and 2 where a sentence of imprisonment has been imposed for a period not less than 3 years, i.e. it is now a crime instead of a crime. Criminal sanctions have also been heightened in connection with this kind of involuntary crime. Previously, there were up to 3 years of imprisonment and after the change - from 6 months to 3 years of imprisonment. The sanction with Article has also been tightened. 156 § 3. If the result of an act referred to in § 1, i.e. serious injury to health, is the death of a person, then the sentence of imprisonment is for 5 years or is a 25-year sentence of imprisonment or a life sentence of imprisonment. In the original record, it was also a crime, but the lower limit has been for 3 years, just like in other crimes. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 189 on deprivation of liberty, a qualified form of crime has been introduced. Where deprivation of liberty concerns a person who is in need of help due to age or health, the perpetrator shall be punishable by imprisonment from 2 to 12 years. Paragraph 3 is also introduced in that provision, which states that if the imprisonment referred to in § 1 and 2a is linked to a particular torment, the sentence of imprisonment shall be for a period of not less than 3 years, i.e. that the offence constitutes a crime. At this stage of the procedure, an amendment to the Article was also introduced. 207 The Penal Code, which refers to physical or mental abuse of the person closest to him or her or her remaining in a permanent or transitory relationship of dependence on the perpetrator. And here is the introduction of a qualifying offence for the benefit of the victim: who physically or mentally abuses a person who is unhelpful by reason of age, mental state or physical condition is punishable by imprisonment from 6 months to 8 years. The new wording was also given § Article 2 and 3 of that provision. 207 Penal Code: If the act referred to in § 1 or 1a is linked to the use of special cruelty, the perpetrator shall be punishable by imprisonment from one year to another 10 years, and if the result of such acts is the execution of the victim for his own life, the sentence of imprisonment shall be 2 to 12 years. About the art. 210 of the Penal Code, that's in § 1 has been amended. This change concerns the party concerned but also the person concerned because of the victim: Who, contrary to the obligation to take care of a minor under the age of 15 or a person who is not able to do so because of his or her mental or physical condition, is punishable by imprisonment from 3 months to 5 years. If the result of the act is the death of a person referred to in paragraph 1, the perpetrator shall be punishable by imprisonment from 2 to 12. The amendment to the Article has also been introduced. 211 concerning a person kidnapped: Who, contrary to the will of a person appointed for care or supervision, kidnaps or arrests a minor under the age of 15 or a person unfit for mental or physical treatment, is punishable by imprisonment from 3 months to 5 years. Art. 240 of the Penal Code received a new sound. § 1 shall have the following content: Who, having a credible message of criminal preparation or attempted or committed a criminal offence referred to in Article. 118, Art. 118a, Art. 120.124, Art. 127, Art. 128, Art. 130, Art. 134, Art. 140, Art. 148, Art. 156, Art. 163, Art. 166, Art. 189, Art. 1 97 § 3 or 4, Art. 198, Art. 200, Art. 252 or terrorist offences, shall not immediately notify the criminal investigation authority, shall be punishable by imprisonment up to the age of 3. In addition, § 2 added § 2a as amended in relation to the version contained in print No 846: It shall not be punishable by the act mentioned in § 1, which has failed to notify of this act. These are changes in the Penal Code. On the other hand, the Juvenile Procedure Act of 26 October 1982 amended the Article. 11 § 3, i.e. determining when conditional suspension may be cancelled and ordering the minor to be placed in a correction facility - inter alia. if, during the trial period, the behaviour of the minor indicates further demoralisation or if the minor is prevented from carrying out the duties or supervision imposed on him, the family court may revoke the conditional suspension as well as at the time of the minor's criminal offence during the trial period referred to in Article. 134, Art. 148 § 1, 2 or 3, Art. 156 § Article 1 or 3, 163 § 1 or 3, Art. 166, Art. 173 § 1 or 3, Art. Article 189 (3), Art. 252 § 1 or 2 and Art. 280 of the Penal Code and the lack of grounds for hearing the case by a court competent under the Code of Criminal Procedure, the family court dismisses the conditional suspension and manages the placing of a minor in a correctional institution. New provisions have also been introduced in Article. 27 § 2 of the Juvenile Procedure Act: A minor may also be placed in a youth hostel if the circumstances in favour of placing him in a correctional facility are revealed and the minor is accused of committing a criminal offence as defined in the same articles as the offence referred to in the provision of the article. 11 § 3. The new wording is also read by Article. 87 § 3a: If a minor commits a criminal offence as referred to in Article. 134, Art. 148 § 1, 2 or 3 et seq. - same as when ordering a minor to be placed in a correction plant pursuant to Article. 27 § 2 - the court competent under the Code of Criminal Procedure shall revoke the conditional release and shall manage the placing of a minor in a correctional institution. When it has been found that a correctional institution has committed certain, same offences, it is what I have already quoted twice, that the family court also decides to transfer a minor to a correctional institution on increased parental supervision or the application of Article. 79 § 4 of the law on criminal proceedings against minors. These court orders shall be subject to appeal. Article 2 is also introduced. 3. This is the consequence of the fact that the act of art. 156 § 3, which was previously a crime, as a result of this amendment becomes a crime, and it was necessary to amend the article. 25 § 1 point 2 of the Code of Criminal Procedure, which refers to the jurisdiction of the district court in rem. That's where point 1 relates to the crime, so the act of art. 156 § 3 The Penal Code fulfils these conditions of crime, and since point 2 was mentioned as an offence, Article 2 of that provision should have been. 156 § 3 remove. The date of entry into force of the Act has also been amended. The original date of her entry was the shortest, 14-day period. In view of the new content of the article, in particular. 240 Penal Code, which is this sanction against those who, having a credible message about the criminal preparation or attempt of these crimes already mentioned by me... Those persons, without immediately notifying the authorities called to prosecute, are subject to criminal liability. Therefore, there was a need to extend the date of entry into force of this law - 3 months from the date of publication - in particular because at that time information about the new criminal penalty for such omission could be made available to our citizens, but also so that the authorities that will implement this law, namely law enforcement bodies, juvenile courts and, of course, the prosecution's office, have time to prepare for the entry into the new criminal code and the law on proceedings against minors in criminal matters. On behalf of the Extraordinary Commission on Changes in Codifications, I ask the High House to adopt this report and ask for the support of this draft submitted by the President of the Republic of Poland. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

There can be no consent to mild treatment of such crimes. Trafficking in human goods, abductions, deprivations of liberty combined with particular torment or finally abandoning children, being completely defenseless, is, in our view, one of the most important criminal problems to which the state should respond with all determination. It is therefore appropriate to take a positive course in strengthening the criminal repression of these categories of offences. In this sense, there is no agreement to the views of a liberal school of criminal law, which rejects general prevention as a deterrent. Not to the children, ladies and gentlemen. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u78	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Invited guests! Club Parliamentary Modern supports the draft amendment of the Penal Code Act and the Juvenile Procedure Act. The aim of the draft law is to increase the level of legal protection of the non-alienable rights of minors with particular regard to persons under the age of 15, minors who are partially or completely incapacitated because of their physical or mental state. It will provide greater protection for minors against a real and potential threat, as well as the effects of third parties' actions. Protecting the citizens of the Republic of Poland is the most important function of the state. Reinforcing the responsibility of perpetrators of crime by introducing an additional type of qualified criminal offence against a person who is ill-advised on grounds of age or health, in particular in the case of parental abductions, may significantly reduce this phenomenon. It is undisputed that human life, health, freedom, the right to respect for family life as well as private life are the most important value in the hierarchy of goods protected by law not only by a single individual but by society as a whole. For their protection it does not matter age, health status, social affiliation, because this protection is given to everyone from the very fact of being human. This protection was established by the Constitution of the Republic of Poland and the Universal Declaration of Human Rights. Therefore, taking into account the data on juvenile delinquency, the tightening of sanctions on offenders seems objectively necessary. However, it should be borne in mind, what is written in the justification for this proposal, that due to the highly repressive nature of criminal law, which could result in profound interference in human life, especially in its freedom and privacy, the legislator should retain restraint in extending the boundaries of criminal law. This means that the goods underlying the type of prohibited act must be really important and deserve special protection. And you can't decide to tighten up penalties, especially in criminal proceedings, without referring to the research required, to analyze the reports of individual institutions, without considering whether raising the penalty will actually make a difference, whether it will improve the situation or not, whether it will deepen social pathology and criminalisation of behaviour. The justification for the need to amend criminal law requires that the proportionality test provided for in Article be carried out on a case-by-case basis. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3rd Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The reasons for the proportionality test are: usefulness, necessity and proportionality of the strict sense. In the opinion of the President of the Republic of Poland, the regulations contained in the proposed project serve to protect minors and pursue objectives that are difficult to achieve otherwise, while at the same time not violating the principle of subsidiarity of criminal law. We trust that the structure of the System Analysis Team's justification in cooperation with the Ombudsman, the Ombudsman, the spokesperson for the rights of the patient and the financial spokesperson gives certainty and confirms the validity of these changes. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u80	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Mr. Spokesman! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party to present our position on the report of the committee on the presidential draft law amending the Penal Code Act and the Act on Juvenile Procedure. Today's project aims to increase the level of protection of minors with particular regard to minors under the age of 15, as well as those who are not able to cope because of their mental or physical state. The project envisages tightening the responsibility for causing serious harm to the minor's health. Today, this act is punishable by a sentence of one to ten years in prison, and where the result of the crime is death - from two years to twelve years. The amendment assumes that it will be from 2 years to 12 years respectively and from 5 years to 15 years in prison. A severe criminal liability is to be threatened for deprivation of liberty of a child under the age of 15 - at least 3 years in prison, for trafficking in children under the age of 15 - from 5 years in prison, for abandonment of a minor or a helpless person - from 3 months to 5 years in the case of death of such a person - from 2 years to 12 years in the case of abduction of a minor or a helpless person - from 3 months to 5 years in the case of death of such a person. The project predicts that the perpetrator of a robbery against a minor or a beating will be treated more severely than anyone who beats an adult. The courts will be required to verify that the offence has been committed to the detriment of the minor in all cases. The project will return to the session of the Sejm after work in committee. The draft of the subcommittee report was tabled by the committee by amendments, inter alia. to tighten the penalty for offences against minors and to change the date of entry into force of the Act. High Izbo! Undoubtedly, life, health, physical integrity and dignity of minors require special care and protection of the state. Children are unable to take care of their subjectivity on their own, and they often remain vulnerable to criminals, while the damage they suffer marks their entire future life. Let us also remember that the welfare of the child is a value protected by the Constitution of the Republic of Poland - in art. 72. 1. So far, criminal penalties against minors have often affected a common sense of justice. According to this sense of punishment for such crimes should be tightened. Once again, however, it cannot be asked whether, in the opinion of the projector, the scale of violence against children and young people will actually be reduced once the penalties for crimes against them have been tightened. Isn't it that it's not harsh, but the inevitability of punishment? Does it really prevent you from committing crimes? A good step in this context, precisely towards the inevitability of the penalty, is introduced in the draft an obligation to notify law enforcement authorities e.g. a pedophile act or beating up a child. A person who has knowledge of such a pedophile act and does not notify it to law enforcement would be in danger of being punished for up to 3 years of imprisonment. This measure should contribute to the reduction of the so-called phenomenon. Social anesthetic, that is, a situation where drama of children remains in four walls of the house, educational institution or colonial center. We have a duty to respond and it is good that this obligation will be reinforced by an additional penalty. We need more action to increase detection and the inevitability of punishment. Only such measures can, in our recognition, effectively limit the scale of the crime the victims of which are minors. Regardless of the above, education and help to parents play a key role. Studies show that the level of acceptance of physical penalties in Poland is decreasing. In our view, this increase in public awareness about the problem of violence against minors does not come from criminalisation of such or other behaviours, but it comes from actions and social campaigns that have been carried out in Poland in recent years. In parallel to criminal law, an information campaign must be launched, otherwise the tightening of penalties alone will not bring the results that we expect. High Izbo! The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I think, like other clubs, is very much in favour of further work and the adoption of this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u84	Dear Marshal, Minister! Gentlemen of Ministers! This concerns the eligibility of offences committed to the detriment of a minor and the absence of criminalisation of criminal conduct contrary to the rights and interests of the child. For this reason, on 7 May 2016, the Ombudsman asked the President to take a legislative initiative to remedy this situation so that the rights of victims of crime, in particular children, are properly protected in Poland by submitting an appropriate project. At this point in the hands of Minister Andrzej Dera, I would like to express my gratitude to the President for taking this initiative and submitting the project to the Sejm. Some solutions have also been added to provide fuller legal protection for children. I welcome the adoption by the committee of the amendment of the Article. 53 § 2 The Penal Code by including in the catalogue of general directives the sentence of the circumstances of committing an offence to the detriment of a person of poor age or health. This is an emphasis on the particular concern that the state should have for these people. The court will have to take into account the fact that the victim is a child when it comes to punishment against the perpetrator. This change concerns all offences committed to the detriment of a minor. It is important to stress that criminal protection is not only done through legislation but also through the obligation to take into account the welfare of a minor in any criminal case. The text adopted by the committee highlights the priority of the new general penalty directive. Another change in the right direction is to increase the level of criminal sanctions for certain offences if the child is injured. This applies to the abandonment, abduction or detention of a minor under the age of 15 or to a person who is in need of assistance. In the view of the Ombudsman of the rights of the child in the case of both of these offences, the perpetrator shall be liable within one month to three years of imprisonment, that penalty shall be inadequate to the social harm of the child and shall not satisfy the protection of the criminal good, not only of family and care, but also of the health, safety and life of the minor up to 15 years. It should be pointed out that the penalty in this amount is at stake, among others. offences such as exerting influence on official activities, obstructing or preventing environmental and labour inspection or illegal border crossing. A comparison of the code protection of these goods with the crime of abandonment, abduction or detention of the child will allow me to leave without comment. During the parliamentary work, the project was completed with the content of the abuse crime. A qualified form has been introduced, through the victim, infirmity due to age or health status. In the view of the Ombudsman, this change complements the presidential solution proposed in the proposal. It will not only increase the protection of such victims and the more severe treatment of the perpetrator, but will allow to diagnose the scale of the crime phenomenon when the child is the victim of abuse, by identifying a new type of qualified abuse crime in the editorial unit. Subsequently, the sanctions raised as a criminal offence to cause severe health disorder should be considered adequate to the harm of a social act. They do justice. The cause of serious injury to health in both primary and qualified forms will rightly be a crime. It will allow for an individual approach to criminal liability, where e.g. the victim is a child or the perpetrator acts with particular cruelty. After all, it is one of the most serious crimes against human life and health, treated so far by the legislator as it is stolen with burglary. The legislation under review also responds to the legitimate criticism of the legal community, as well as the public and the media, which have repeatedly expressed their views on this subject, commenting on the appalling acts of violence that have resulted in a serious disability or death of the child, indicating that the perpetrators of such crimes are being treated too gently. They will make a more adversarial punishment by broadening the range of cases where the penalty is appropriate to the reality of a particular case, by means of a criminal response, while preserving the possibility of individualising its dimension. I would like to thank the Members for their sympathy for the proposal to introduce a new type of qualified imprisonment where it concerns an unhelpful person due to age or health, and for tightening the criminal liability for abandoning a minor under the age of 15 or a helpless person when the result is the death of the victim. Currently, the perpetrator is responsible for 6 months to 8 years of imprisonment, and so is fraud. A separate discussion requires the proposed addition of a catalogue of offences for which the perpetrator has knowledge of their involvement and does not inform law enforcement authorities. It's an art. 240 § 1 of the Penal Code. In this respect, the project provides for the inclusion of the most serious offences, such as serious injury to health, the most serious rape, i.e. action with special cruelty, rape of minors under 15 years of age, collective rape, rape of the nearest person, use of helplessness, disability and sexual intercourse with minors. All these crimes are characterised by a high degree of social harm. In my view, a person who has knowledge of a serious crime committed against a child and does not inform the authorities called to prosecute him becomes co-responsible and should suffer the consequences of his failure to do so. Let us not forget that criminal law plays a special role in the system of law, primarily as a protection and justice function. In the end, it is always for the court to decide whether one of the surroundings could have real knowledge of the crime committed and did nothing about it. In the current Article. 240 § 1 of the Penal Code The focus is on the protection of the State and the interests of the Republic. On the other hand, it does not provide adequate criminal protection provided for in the existing legal situation, in particular as regards the protection of children from sexual crime, i.e. behaviour which seriously undermines the rights and welfare of the child. Protecting the child through the fight against crime aimed at his well-being should be a top priority. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u108	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I present on behalf of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights a report on the Government's draft law amending the Law on the System of General Courts, prints No 1181 and No 1286. As indicated by the applicant, the project aims to organise the system of operation of the directors of the courts in order to improve the efficiency of staff management in the general courts with regard to the positions of director and vice-director. In particular, the amendments concern the rules governing the appointment and dismissal of the directors of the courts and their alternates, the rules governing the duties of the directors of the courts, as well as the taking by the Presidents of the courts of activities with financial implications. In other words, it is a matter of emphasising the separation of what is judgment in courts from what is administered in courts. The Sejm at its 34th meeting on 26 January 2017 pursuant to Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the draft law to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights for consideration. The committee was held on 26 January. The Supreme Court and the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Treasury presented written opinions on the draft law. The committee was attended by the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Justice, Patrick Jak together with his colleagues, as well as employees of the Sejm Chancellery, the Registry of the Justice Committee and the Legislative Office. The result of the committee's work is the draft law included in the report in print no. 1286. At the meeting of the committee, the draft was presented by the Secretary of State Patryk Jak. Then a discussion took place. All the regulations of the project have been systematically discussed and adopted at this meeting. In the opinions submitted and in the loss of discussion, doubts were raised as to the accuracy of the submission and some of the regulations of the project. As a result of the discussion, due to the amendments notified, some of the doubts were eliminated or mitigated. Substantive amendments and legislative amendments were adopted. The substantive amendments concerned the withdrawal of the rules excluding the director of the court from the judicial authorities and the flexibility of the appointment and dismissal of the directors in the regional courts. Some of the amendments tabled were rejected, mainly on the grounds that they did not fulfil the purpose of the Act as formulated by the applicants. Their content is in principle in line with the requests made by minorities, which are presented in print no. 1286. Nine Members voted in favour of the bill, three Members opposed, and no one abstained. The Committee on Justice and Human Rights, after considering the draft law, asks the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law as proposed in the committee's report in print no. 1286. According to Art. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, at the request of the applicants in the persons of Barbara Dolniak, Kamila Gasiuk-Pihowicz, Robert Kropiwnicki and Marcin Święcicki, the committee presents six minority proposals. I would like to thank the ladies and gentlemen and all the experts and committee members for their cooperation. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The media has repeatedly reported in recent days, in recent weeks of scandalous cases of criminal behaviour of judges in public space, in the space of ordinary human behaviour, and paradoxically this law has no connection with this, but certainly this legislation, these legislative changes should also have an impact on the ordering of standards for the performance of the judges themselves, as well as by the administration which directs the courts. The purpose of the bill submitted is to clearly separate the functions of the judicial administration, which fall within the competence of the President of the Court, relating to the performance of judicial tasks, from administrative activities falling within the competence of the Director of the Court. The fundamental change in this respect is a new definition of the status of directors of the courts, whose authority will be the Minister of Justice. In comparison with the original wording of the draft law, an amendment was proposed by the committee leaving the director of the court as the body of the court. The wording of the Article has also been amended. 21a § 1, according to which the position of the head of the court in a regional court is not compulsory and may be established where this is justified by organisational reasons, and in particular by the size of the court measured by the number of judicial, assesor and referenda posts and the staff employed therein or by the distance from the district court. However, no amendment to the amended Article has been made. 21a § 3, according to which the President of the Court will perform labour law activities in relation to the Director of the Court, except those reserved for the Minister of Justice. In our view - as I also mentioned at the first reading of the bill - it would be appropriate to include a catalogue of these activities reserved for the Minister of Justice, which he would carry out in relation to the directors of the courts. Such an enumerative catalogue would make it clear - contrary to the suggestions made by some legal circles, also due to certain concerns as to how the court will function as an institution under the amended law, as well as some political environments - that the aim of the project is solely to supervise the minister's administrative activities of the courts, rather than to influence or influence the case law of the courts. Given that this law in our view is necessary and will de facto bring a good separation of administrative functions from judicial functions, Members of the Free and Solidarnia Members, despite the absence of this record of the catalogue of activities reserved for the Minister of Justice, are ready to support the submitted [[♪ The bell ♪]] The bill's draft. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u140	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, I have the honour to present a report on the Government's draft law amending the Law on the Prevention of Pollution from Ships and certain other laws, print No 1194. The project aims to implement European Union law, concerns the implementation into Polish law of certain EU rules on maritime spatial planning, ship recycling, monitoring, reporting and verification of carbon dioxide emissions from maritime transport and sampling and reporting on sulphur content of marine fuels. The first reading of the project took place at the 34th meeting of the Sejm on 26 January this year. The project was then addressed to the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation for consideration, with a recommendation to submit a report by 21 February this year. The Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation at its meeting on 8 February 2017 considered this draft law. In the course of the discussion, amendments were tabled to clarify the provisions of the draft law and the legislative and editorial nature. In its vote, the committee voted unanimously in favour of the report on the draft law. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation recommends that the Sejm adopt the report of the committee in print No 1264. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u141	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Ms Dorota Arciszewska-Mielewczyk will speak on behalf of the Parliamentary Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u142	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I will take the floor on the draft law amending the Law on the Prevention of Pollution from Ships and certain other laws, print Nos 1194 and 1264. The purpose of the draft law amending the Law on the Prevention of Pollution from Ships and certain other laws is to implement certain provisions of the European Union's normative acts and international organisations facilitating the activities of maritime sector entities in the field of maritime spatial planning, ship recycling, monitoring, reporting and verification of carbon dioxide emissions from maritime transport and the prevention of... sorry, and sampling and reporting on sulphur content of marine fuels. The project contains a number of references to the international legal acts mentioned in the explanatory memorandum due to the need to apply uniform legal and organisational arrangements to the entire system of supervision of the activities of maritime administrations. The bill contains proposals for amendments to the Law on the Prevention of Pollution from Ships, the Law on Maritime Areas of the Republic of Poland and Maritime Administration, the Law on Ports and Seaports, the Act on Environmental Protection, the Law on Maritime Safety, the Law on Waste and on the Amendment of the Law on Maritime Safety and the Law on Maritime Labour. The proposed solutions include: the introduction of the IT system THETIS-EU which will allow the collection of ship inspection data in the framework of the enforcement of the sulphur directive and the Directive on port reception facilities for ship-generated waste and cargo residues; the introduction of a definition of planning and spatial planning of marine areas of internal waters, the territorial sea and the exclusive economic zone; the introduction of the period of validity of qualification examinations and certificates for training for seafarers and fishermen and the publication of lists of examiners of the Central Maritime Examination Commission; the implementation of new provisions forced by amendments to the International Convention on the Safety of Life at Sea, the SOLAS Convention, concerning the need to verify the gross mass of containers loaded on ships. These provisions aim to eliminate the underweight of containers in transport documents compared to the actual weight of containers, which has consequently caused serious maritime disasters; regulation of ship recycling activities. Implementation into Polish law of current EU requirements will establish better conditions for the current control and reporting procedures related to ship inspections and will result in better supervision of compliance with legal requirements for the protection of the marine environment. The provisions of the draft law will also ensure that administrative authorities effectively carry out the tasks of supervising maritime operators. The proposed rules will not result in financial burdens on the state budget. Financial penalties for non-compliance with ship recycling requirements, the use on ships of non-compliant sulphur fuels and the non-compliance with monitoring and reporting obligations for CO2 emissions from maritime transport may generate additional revenue for the State budget. Similarly, additional revenue may be affected by checks on shippers carried out to approve the use of method two for verifying the gross weight of containers loaded onto ships to the State budget. By means of the proposed legislation, shipbuilding operators will be able to undertake a fully-fledged and standardised activity in the field of safe and environmentally sound ship recycling. This will certainly contribute to the development of this sector in our country by ensuring the conditions for ship recycling on a competitive basis while guaranteeing the right regulatory framework. The Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, of course, recommends that the Sejm adopt the report of the committee in print No 1264. The Law and Justice Club is also in favour of this opinion and is in favour of this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u146	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a statement on behalf of the Kukiz Club15 on the government draft law amending the Law on the Prevention of Pollution from Ships and some other laws. I am referring to the laws: maritime areas of the Republic of Poland and maritime administration, ports and marinas, environmental law, maritime safety, waste, maritime safety, maritime work. The project aims to bring Polish law into line with some international documents. If we take account of the level of pollution in the Baltic Sea, we should realise that it is in our national interest to adopt those regulations that will help prevent pollution by ships. The level of pollution of Baltic waters continues to rise. This has a negative impact on our economy. Fish are slowly beginning to disappear from the Polish Coast, and the number of fish caught every year is decreasing. I would add here that we must take into account our tourism, which may also be in question in the future. Implementing documents, such as the update of the International Convention on the Safety of Life at Sea, the European Parliament and Council Directives: establishing a framework for maritime spatial planning, on ship recycling, on monitoring, reporting and verification of carbon dioxide emissions from maritime transport, provide for further Union tightenings. However, if it weren't for them, we should still put in place additional regulations on the protection of our Baltic Sea. Oil pollution in the Baltic Sea occurs from various sources in many different forms, from volatile gases to dense liquid sludges. The most widespread are mineral oil spills from ships and offshore oil platforms, airborne volatile hydrocarbons from fossil fuel combustion and exhaust gas from combustion vehicles, diluted oil spills from cities and coastal industries, hydrocarbon vapours from oil distribution terminals and filling stations. Oil pollution reaches the marine environment in small areas, but can often be spread by far-reaching winds and currents, causing further serious pollution along the entire coastline. Seabirds and marine bottom ecosystems are the most at risk here. It appears that the introduction of the definition of the fuel circulation system and THETIS-EU is intended to be a tool to improve the performance of inspection duties, including the choice of ships for inspection and the electronic reporting of its results. Polish regulations will include regulations on the position of the national administrator and the definition of its tasks. The Kukiz Posel Club15, recognising that the Baltic Sea is our national good, supports all actions that will lead to its protection. We therefore support this draft law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

If this investment were carried out as planned, the channel in the Polish part of the Vistula mine would be ready. Only now that the rule of law and justice has been taken over again is there political will and acceptance for this investment. The Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping was also established, for which this investment is a priority. The draft law currently under discussion aims to improve the preparation and implementation of investments from the state budget of PLN 880 million pursuant to Resolution 57/2016 of the Council of Ministers of 24 May 2016 on the establishment of a multiannual programme under the name "Building of the Waterway linking Zalew Wiślany with the Gulf of Gdansk for the years 2016-2022. The construction of the shipping channel will be carried out because of the overriding public interest of ensuring security in the region and free access to ports located on the Vistula River. The Act serves the implementation of a new waterway, and the experience gained from the construction of land roads indicates that focusing in a single regulation of administrative and factual decisions will result in efficient and reasonable implementation of this investment in time. The rapid and effective implementation of the new waterway requires a shortening of administrative procedures and consolidation of decisions currently issued separately in a single administrative act. The proposed special act introduces procedural facilitations and regulates the powers of individual decision-making bodies. The provisions of the proposed law will apply to entities involved in the implementation of investments carried out on the basis of a specialty, i.e. to the Director of the Maritime Office in Gdynia and to voivodships - including Pomorskie Voivodeship in cooperation with Warmia-Mazury Voivodeship, because the investment will be carried out in the area of two voivodships - as the bodies issuing the decision to allow the implementation of the above mentioned. investment in access infrastructure. This investment, which is very important because of our security, sovereignty and defence issues, is equally important to provide an additional boost to the economic development of all municipalities located on the Vistula River. The construction of the shipping channel according to analyses carried out on behalf of the Maritime Authority in Gdynia will create about 3,000. jobs initially in the implementation of this investment and then in the operation of the channel, deepening of the water tracks, as well as in ports and marinas located [[♪ The bell ♪]] in Elbląg, Suchach, Tolkkick, Frombork, Stegnie, Arts and Maritime Krynica. It is an opportunity for economic and tourist development of the whole region, and above all for the independence and sovereignty of Poland. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support the government's draft law on investments in the construction of a waterway connecting Zalev Wiślany with the Gulf of Gdańsk. Thank you very much. February 8th this year a first reading of the draft was held at the Health Committee at which the project was considered. The committee's report is in print No 1283. The aim of the contested law is to lay down the rules for the establishment of a programme of specialised training in the field of health protection and accreditation for specific training, as well as for the supervision of the implementation and control of the training and the conditions and modalities for obtaining the title of a specialist in the field of health care, including the accession and holding of specialist training, as well as the organisation and conduct of the State Health Specialization Examination. The content of the proposed proposal has been regulated so far by the Regulation of the Minister of Health, but since that Regulation expired on 1 July 2016, the Minister of Health has undertaken to regulate these issues in the Act. This is actually a transfer to the law that has been regulated so far in the regulation, Those who did specialization in a non-medical profession and have completed this specialization after 1 July will only be able to take the exam on the basis of the present law. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u205	Thank you, sir. Mr Mark Hok, please take the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u206	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club regarding the government draft law on obtaining the title of specialist in the areas applicable to health protection. The Government's draft law on the acquisition of the title of specialist in the areas applicable to health protection results from the need to replace the regulation of the Minister of Health of 30 September 2002 on the acquisition of the title of specialist in the areas applicable to health care, which has become obsolete as a result of the replacement of the Law on health care establishments by the Act of 15 April on medicinal activities. Specializations could only be started on the basis of existing legislation until 1 July 2016. From now on, there is no legal basis to allow specialisation to begin. There is therefore a quite urgent need to regulate these rules for obtaining the title of specialist, and actually the draft of this new statutory regulation is largely a transfer of the content of the regulation to the proposed bill. Of course, we should also point out the shortcomings that are in this law, and perhaps one of the most important is that this law has a fundamental disadvantage, which is that it does not contain a list of the basic areas applicable to health protection. If we decide to regulate the statutory rules for obtaining the title of specialist, the basic legal matter of such regulation should be the definition of the areas to which this law applies. The draft introduces technical provisions, but there is no specific basic scope of these areas, and there are only a dozen that I will allow me to mention, i.e. epidemiology, medical physics, medical engineering, health promotion and health education, clinical psychology, neurology, public health, environmental health, toxicology, microbiology, pharmaceutical industry, radiopharmaceutical, surdologopedia. In addition, psychosexuality and clinical embryology are added. So that the disadvantage of this project is that the scope of the entity law is not clarified, while at the same time very detailed regulation of issues which could be transferred to the law implementing regulation. The project also does not implement the frequently submitted postulate for the development and adoption of a law on health professions. So that there will still be no definition of legal health professions or their statutory qualifications. Despite these reservations, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will vote in favour of the proposed law, because hundreds of people expect to be able to submit documentation for specialization in order to carry out these specializations, because on 1 July 2016 this possibility ended. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u207	Thank you. And Mr Jerzy Kozłowski, please take the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u208	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I have the honour to present our position on the governmental draft law on obtaining the title of specialist in the areas applicable to health care, print Nos 1165 and 1283. The draft law sets out the terms and conditions for obtaining the title of specialist in the areas applicable to health care. The title of a specialist will be obtained after completion of specialization training or after recognition of the existing professional or scientific achievements as equivalent to the implementation of the specialization programme and the sentence of the State Specialization Examination in a given field, in short PESoz, e.g. After completing the training and passing the exam, the psychologist will be able to become a specialist in clinical psychology, a speechpedist - a specialist in neurologopedia and a physicist - in medical physics. Rules have been laid down for the establishment of the training programme, for the accreditation to conduct training, for the organisation and conduct of the examination, as well as for the supervision of the implementation of the training, including its control, as well as for the conditions and modalities for obtaining the title of specialist. Specialization training will take place on the basis of training programmes developed by a team of experts appointed by the Director of the Postgraduate Medical Training Centre, in short the CMKP, and approved by the Minister of Health, which is the same as before. Training will also be available for training units that have been accredited by the Director of the CMKP. In order to obtain accreditation, the entity concerned shall submit to the Director by means of a system to monitor the training of medical staff an application for accreditation in electronic form, signed by a qualified electronic signature or signed by a trusted ePUAP profile. In order to qualify for training, the voivode will conduct the selection procedure from 16 December to 15 January and from 1 June to 30 June of each year, which will include an assessment of compliance with the conditions set out in the Act. If the number of persons meeting the conditions is higher than the number of seats, the competition procedure will be conducted. To date, the qualification procedure has been conducted in training units involving voivodships and voivodship consultants from specific fields. The control of the implementation of specialization training carried out by the training unit will be part of the duties of the control team. The control team shall be appointed by the Director of the CMKP. The scope of the control activities and the powers of the controllers, as well as the control documentation and related procedures have been defined. The consequences for the training unit were also indicated. In the event of a finding of irregularities during checks and failure to follow-up recommendations within the prescribed time limit, the entity shall lose its accreditation and be deleted from the list of entities holding such an accreditation. The rules of procedure and the submission of PESoz were defined in detail. It has been proposed to change the admission to this examination. New solutions for passing exams are designed to avoid problems so far. For the exam, persons who took a specialization and submitted a specialization card to the voivodship centre together with an application for admission to the exam may be admitted. The voivodship centre will pass them to the Centre for Medical Examinations once the formal conditions have been fulfilled. The provisions did not specify how the missing elements of the training programme could be supplemented. The project provides for a wide use of electronic systems in the process of obtaining a title. First of all, a system for monitoring the training of medical workers is envisaged. The system aims to accelerate the training process, ensure transparency of procedures and allow the replacement of paper circulation of electronic documents. However, we have some comments on the project. These are three comments. The draft does not specify the scope of this law. Also in the article. Article 27 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 5 is something that may raise doubts about compliance with the Code of Administrative Procedure. K.p.a. provides for a two-way procedure, and this article refers to the possibility of asking the voivod to verify the decision, i.e. such a scheme: decision - verification. [[♪ The bell ♪]] And for us there are unclear provisions regarding the recognition of the acquis as equivalent - Article. 28. Despite these reservations, the Kukiz Club will vote in favour of this bill. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u232	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the club Law and Justice I have the honour to present the position of the club on the government draft law amending the Act on Competitions of Doctors and Dentist and the Act on Collection, Storage and Transplanting Cells, Tissues and Organs. The draft Act amending the Act on Competitions of Doctors and Dentist and on Collection, Storage, Transplantation of Cells, Tissues and Organs proposes the transfer of the current provisions concerning the determination of permanent irreversible cessation of brain function, i.e. brain death, and irreversible stopping of circulation prior to organ procurement - from the so-called. a transplant bill to the Act on Doctor and Dentist Competitions. Introduce these provisions and include them in Article. 43 and Article 43 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 298, 26.10.2012, p. 1). 43a The Act on Competitions of Doctor and Dentist, together with the provisions on the determination of death, will increase knowledge of these particular types of human death, which becomes the first initiating link in the process of organ transplantation. This is a very important matter. Another important good change in the statutory provisions is the resignation of the three-person panel on permanent irreversible cessation of brain function, i.e. brain death, which is currently appointed by the head of the medical entity. The difficulties in completing the three-member committee, both of the permanent composition and of the appointed medical director's order for a specific decision, have repeatedly led to the lack of a death sentence and consequently the loss of the donor and, as a last resort, the opportunity for the recipient has been lost. The unanimous statement by two doctors of experts of permanent irreversible brain failure, as well as the irreversible stopping of the circulation prior to organ donation, is consistent with the procedure for the determination of brain stem death in most European countries, including the United States and Canada. The admission to the death of additional specialists: neonatology, pediatrics, paediatric neurology and children's cardiology, also emergency medicine, internal diseases, cardiology will increase the number of people who will be able to procedate, which will make it much easier in some hospitals, both those high-specialist paediatric clinics and small county hospitals, even to rule out the death of the brain stem and thus prepare a potential donor. The Act also introduces the publication by the Minister responsible for Health, by means of a notice, in the Official Journal of the Republic of Poland, of the manner and criteria of establishing permanent irreversible cessation of brain function and irreversible stopping of circulation prior to organ collection. The method of judgment and criteria for the determination of death in these cases will be determined according to current medical knowledge by specialists in the relevant fields of medicine and will be evaluated at least once every 5 years for compliance with current medical knowledge. Until the announcement of the method and criteria for determining brain death and irreversible cardiac arrest prior to organ procurement, the existing rules apply, which is also positive. In the Act on the collection, storage and transplantation of cells, tissues and organs there are provisions that allow the collection of cells, tissues and organs to permanently cease brain function, the determination of death as a result of irreversible cardiac arrest. There are also provisions to prohibit participation in transplant operations by a doctor who determines death due to permanent irreversible cessation of brain function and irreversible arrest of the circulation prior to organ donation, as well as the collection of cells, tissues and organs from the deceased if the doctor has found death of the person. Legal regulation by amendments to the Act on Competitions and Dentist Practices and the Transplantation Act should further develop transplantology as an effective treatment for extreme organ failure [[♪ The bell ♪]] and increase the number of organ transplants and tissue transplants. The Law and Justice Club will vote in favour of this law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u236	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Kukiz Club15, I have the honour to present my position on the government draft law amending the Act on Competitions of Doctors and Dentist and the Act on Collection, Storage and Transplanting of Cells, Tissues and Organs, Seym Printing Nos 1212 and 1284. The main objective of the bill is to transfer from the transplant law to the Act on Competitions of Doctors and Dentists the provisions concerning the determination of death as a result of permanent, irreversible cessation of brain function and irreversible cardiac arrest. The diagnosis of death is one of the many activities that every doctor does. In the opinion of the designer, the way in which the rules have been placed so far gives the impression that the judgment of death in the above mentioned. cases are carried out by physicians in order to collect cells, tissues and organs as soon as possible. This also affects doctors themselves, thus creating a barrier to the development of transplantology in Poland. Therefore, such rules should not be directly combined with the provisions of the transplant law. This position is also presented by the National transplant board, which consists of experts from various fields and is an advisory and advisory body of the Minister responsible for Health. In the amended article. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Act on Competitions of Doctor and Dentist is a general rule that a doctor may determine death on the basis of personal examinations and findings. A new article has been added. 43a concerning a procedure for the detection of brain death or irreversible cardiac arrest prior to organ donation. It has been established that this must be stated unanimously by two specialist doctors with a second degree of specialization or a specialist title, including one specialist in anaesthesia and intensive care or neonatology, and the other in the field of emergency medicine, internal diseases, cardiology, paediatric cardiology or pediatrics. It should be stressed that the collection of organs after diagnosis of cardiac arrest must be performed within a very short period of time. Therefore, this diagnosis differs from the routine diagnosis of cardiopulmonary arrest, and due to potential doubts or possible allegations that a person who wishes to lead to organ procurement will make such a diagnosis of cardiopulmonary arrest in most countries on a double basis. The project also introduced the principle that permanent irreversible brain failure should be identified by two doctors, including one specialist in anesthesiology and intensive therapy or neonatology, and the other specialist in neurology, paediatric neurology or neurosurgery. This solution is applied in most European countries, as well as in the United States and Canada. The introduction of a neonatologist and a paediatric neurologist will enable the decision to be taken in neonatal branches run by neonatologists, since according to the existing legislation, the paediatric neurologist is not entitled to rule in this regard. This is a very good bill for the development of Polish transplantology, so we have no doubt as the Poselski Kukiz Club15 to support this bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u248	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Clarifications concern provisions relating to patient rights, the powers of the ombudsman and the responsibilities and rules of operation of the voivodship panel on medical events. Such information will have the right to require a doctor, a nurse, a nurse and other professionals. After changes to the bill, the patient will decide whether a loved one is present during the course of the health benefit. A very important change is the patient's right to treat pain regardless of its origin, the patient's age and the place where he found himself. The law must be implemented in accordance with the indications of current medical knowledge. Until now, which is very important, under the rules, only patients in terminal condition have been entitled to such legislation. I want to point out that this is a good change for the patient. The Act also contains regulations on health data related to the confidentiality of documentation. The Act will clarify the rules of access to medical records, among others. it is expressly stated that persons pursuing a medical profession process the data contained in the medical records under the law and that the other persons, on the basis and within the limits of the authorisation granted by the controller of that data. These data are confidential even after the patient's death. Another amendment to the Act is to clarify the powers of the ombudsman. A provision has been added which will enable the patient to lodge a complaint with the administrative court if he does not agree with the decision of the Ombudsman. The proceedings before the voivodship committee for ruling on medical events have been unified. It will now be up to the voivodships to inform the Minister of Health and the ombudsman about the work of the voivodship committee on medical incidents. The Health Committee has given a positive opinion on the bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The proposed law, in addition to the amendment mentioned in the title of the Act, also introduces certain amendments to the Act on Medical Chambers and the Act on the Self-Government of Nurses and Midwives. The changes proposed in the government submission implement a number of important requests made by patients for many years, by patient organisations, as well as by medical staff, including many times indicated in the reports of the Supreme Auditorium. The project is moving, among others. repeatedly raised in various documents the problem of patient's right to health information and of proposed and possible diagnostic and therapeutic methods, as well as the consequences of the application or omission of certain procedures and of the results of treatment and foreseeable prognosis. The Act also regulates the right of minors' patients to obtain information about their health and to express or not agree to the use of specific diagnostics and therapy. The problem of proper information about treatment is of great importance for patients, for their subjective treatment and respect for their conscious, expressed through the information obtained by their will. However, the wording in Article is unclear. 9 in Amendment 2 to point (a) a. that the person providing the information is to do so in accordance with his/her powers. This record can cause many misunderstandings and difficulties of interpretation. After all, many medical professionals generally participate in the treatment of each patient, and only one of them, I hope, is responsible for the entire course of the treatment process, so only those who treat and are responsible for the entire treatment process can competently provide the patient with such information. Due to the exceptional importance of the words for the patient, such information must be complete and sufficient. I would ask the Minister to explain this problem. Another important regulation in this law concerns the right of the patient to treat pain. The law regulates the right of all patients to treat pain. The statutory guarantee of access to pain treatment in all conditions related to disease is an important change, needed and very expected by patients. The legal granting of this right must also be followed by actions which will ensure that those suffering from pain have effective access to a pain management clinic and the necessary assistance in all cases to treat pain according to current medical indications. The proposed law also contains provisions on the proper protection of sensitive data, which are data on the patient's health, and on his/her personal data. The implementation of this objective is to serve as laid down in the law on the procedure for conducting, storing, processing and making available information on the process of diagnosis and treatment of the patient. It should be stressed that this problem requires urgent solutions in view of the need to protect health information and the course of treatment. The proposed provisions significantly increase the effectiveness and quality of protection, the responsibility of the manufacturing, storage and provision of health information and the course of treatment. The Act also regulates the treatment of medical records after the entity has ceased to provide medical services. This is important for the protection of sensitive data, as well as for the continuity of patient treatment and evaluation of current procedures. The Act also lays down rules for the provision of medical records after the patient's death. The law, which must be stressed, provides for considerable penalties for failure to comply with the obligations relating to medical documentation. I would also like to point out that this law contains provisions on the operation of the Regional Commissions on Medical Events and Medical Commissions, which clarify the way in which committees are set up. In several articles, the Act clarifies and supplements the provisions relating to the ombudsman by assigning the Ombudsman new tasks, specifying in detail the appearances of the ombudsman in the event of identified breaches of the patient's rights. I would like to stress that the proposed law contains very important sequencing solutions and works in favour of the patient. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Therefore, the Law and Justice Club will vote in favour. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u254	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! According to this project, each patient will have the right to treat pain, as under the current rules access to these benefits to relieve pain and other sufferings is granted to the patient in terminal condition. The patient will be able to obtain information on the state of his or her health, proposed diagnostic and therapeutic methods, results and prognosis from a medical professional, e.g. From a paramedic. At this point, you can only get this information from your doctor. The patient will decide whether or not a loved one is present during the provision of health care. The purpose of the amendment is to clarify the wording. The current rule only says that such a person may be present, it does not refer to the patient's will. The catalogue of persons to whom the health care provider makes medical records available is extended to medical professionals authorised by a medical establishment, members of hospital infection control teams and professionals, other than doctors or nurses, midwives, in connection with the procedure for obtaining quality certificates, e.g. like physiotherapists, lab diagnes or medical rescuers. The original medical records will only be issued at the request of public authorities and courts and if a copy could delay the provision of health care, which would endanger the health or life of the patient. In addition to the acknowledgement of receipt, the original shall be returned immediately after use. This will also apply to X-rays. The paper documentation shall be made available for inspection at the place where the health benefits are provided, with the possibility of taking notes or photographs, and by making copies, copies, extracts or printouts. Documentation in electronic form is to be made available via electronic communication or on an IT data medium. The next thing that the bill regulates is the reduction of the maximum fee for making one page copies of medical records from 84 gr to 30 gr. Another point: The referral to a study that has not been used by the patient will be kept for 2 years from the end of the calendar year in which it was issued. There is currently no regulation in this area. The medical records intended for destruction may be issued to the patient, his statutory representative or his authorised person when the period of storage of the records ends. Financial penalties are provided for health care providers who have not complied with their obligations to secure medical records and after the end of their business. A provision has also been made to allow the patient to lodge a complaint with the administrative court to decide on the Ombudsman's rights in individual cases. The Poselski Kukiz Club15 will vote without any doubt in favour of this bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u278	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! The Public Finance Commission at its meeting on 21 February considered a government bill amending the Individual Income Tax Act - it is print No 1258. The committee's report is in print No 1301. In the current legal state, taxpayers of income tax on natural persons earning income exclusively from payers or pension bodies who wanted to benefit from deductions for rehabilitation expenses, child relief or the possibility to transfer 1% the tax payable to the chosen public benefit organisation shall be required to draw up and submit a tax return to the tax office. The complicated structure of the statement and the annexes showing reliefs often gives taxpayers trouble to fill them out on their own. The purpose of the proposed change is, inter alia, to: the release of tax payers from income tax on individuals earning income exclusively from payers, including pension authorities, from self-made tax returns, allowing taxable persons who are cleared by pension authorities to transfer 1% tax for a public benefit organisation without the need to provide a tax return, reducing the cost of collecting personal income tax. First of all, it is about reducing the costs of printing, handling and storing paper versions of documents. Of course, this also has a positive impact on environmental protection. During the committee work, two amendments were tabled, which were accepted. The meeting was attended by the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance, Minister Leszek Skiba. A number of problems have been identified in the areas covered by the Directive, which, in view of the need to ensure the proper coherence of EU law, require the adaptation or even the introduction of new regulations by the Member States of the European Union. In the housing mortgage market in the European Union, cases of irresponsible lending and borrowing and irresponsible behaviour have been identified. In particular, incomparable, incomplete and unclear advertising material, lack of adequate assessment of the suitability of the products offered to the customer's needs and insufficient verification of the customer's creditworthiness were indicated. Among other problematic issues, particular attention has been paid to the inefficiency, inconsistencies or even the lack of systems governing the activities of credit intermediaries offering loans related to residential property. The bill provides for a comprehensive regulation of loans related to real estate. At the same time, the proposal provides for amendments to the Consumer Credit Act which clarify certain of its provisions. The proposed regulations should contribute to the comparability and transparency of lenders' offers and to the consumer's position in the consumer's credit and residential property markets. In view of the above, it therefore appears appropriate to amend the Consumer Credit Act accordingly. The proposed amendment therefore aims to ensure that the provisions of the Act are as precise, unambiguous and transparent as possible. This amendment also appears necessary primarily from the consumer's point of view. As an example, it is possible to clarify the rules on pre-contractual information obligations, which will strengthen the consumer's position on the market by increasing the comparability of business offers. The aim of the project is to ensure a uniform interpretation of the Consumer Credit Act in accordance with the 2008 Directive and to take into account the concerns of the European Commission. In view of the above, the amendment of the Consumer Credit Act will strengthen consumer position in the consumer credit market, increase transparency and precision of consumer credit rules and increase comparability and transparency of lenders' offers. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-22-sejm-36-1.u306	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I will present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law on mortgage credit and on the supervision of mortgage intermediaries and printing agents no. 1210. In the area of credit, this must also be mentioned, we still have numerous legal gaps, which consequently lead to irregularities and even pathology. A clear example is the so-called loans. It's french. It has become necessary to specify: incorrect information, sneaky, unreadable provisions concerning credit costs or securing the return of borrowings and loans. The project faces, removes these irregularities. The main objective here is to implement the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 4 February 2014 on consumer credit agreements relating to residential immovable property. The project introduces comprehensive mortgage arrangements, including supervision of mortgage intermediaries and agents providing mortgage related services. At the same time, the project amends the Consumer Credit Act, eliminating e.g. doubts about the terms used. Particular emphasis is placed on information issues. The creditor, mortgage broker and agent shall ensure that all credit information is unambiguous, reliable, understandable and visible and does not mislead the consumer. This information must already be at the advertising and marketing stage as well as before the conclusion of the credit agreement. The bill provides for and contains e.g. a valid information form which the creditor or credit intermediary or agent is required to provide to the consumer on a durable medium before the contract is signed. It also contains detailed rules in force when withdrawing from the credit agreement, as well as in the case of early repayment of the loan or debt restructuring. In this respect, we must say that we are still seeing many irregularities at present, which raise a number of controversies resulting from these irrational and unfavourable solutions for customers, proposed both in different forms and by different lenders. The project also regulates in very detail the status of mortgage intermediaries and agents supervised by the Financial Supervision Commission. It is the Financial Supervision Commission that will keep, that is also quite important, in the IT system a register of credit intermediaries: mortgage intermediaries, agents, as well as consumer credit intermediaries, and a second register, also mandatory, a register of non-banks lending to consumers and consumer credit intermediaries. The work of the committees and subcommittees has introduced numerous legislative and clarification changes, as well as substantive changes, often expected by financial institutions, associations and foundations. From their conclusion, among others. the penalties applicable to institutions and persons who do not comply with the rules contained in the project have been increased. To sum up, the project is good and the club of the Polish People's Party will vote in favour of this project. Thank you. [[Applause]]
